(This also means that we do not have to\n4936|   have special versions of these functions for when OCTETS_PER_BYTE_POWER\n4937|   is non-zero).\n4938|\n4939|   If the 7-bit values were to be packed into N-bit bytes (where N > 8)\n4940|   we would then have to consider whether multiple, successive LEB128\n4941|   values should be packed into the bytes without padding (bad idea) or\n4942|   whether each LEB128 number is padded out to a whole number of bytes.\n4943|   Plus you have to decide on the endianness of packing octets into a\n4944|   byte.  */\n4945|\n4946|/* Return the size of a LEB128 value in bytes.  */\n4947|\n4948|static inline unsigned int\n4949|sizeof_sleb128 (offsetT value)\n4950|{\n4951|  int size = 0;\n4952|  unsigned byte;\n4953|\n4954|  do\n4955|    {\n4956|      byte = (value & 0x7f);\n4957|      /* Sadly, we cannot rely on typical arithmetic right shift behaviour.\n4958|\t Fortunately, we can structure things so that the extra work reduces\n4959|\t to a noop on systems that do things \"properly\".  */\n4960|      value = (value >> 7) | ~(-(offsetT)1 >> 7);\n4961|      size += 1;\n4962|    }\n4963|  while (!(((value == 0) && ((byte & 0x40) == 0))\n4964|\t   || ((value == -1) && ((byte & 0x40) != 0))));\n4965|\n4966|  return size;\n4967|}\n4968|\n4969|static inline unsigned int\n4970|sizeof_uleb128 (valueT value)\n4971|{\n4972|  int size = 0;\n4973|\n4974|  do\n4975|    {\n4976|      value >>= 7;\n4977|      size += 1;\n4978|    }\n4979|  while (value != 0);\n4980|\n4981|  return size;\n4982|}\n4983|\n4984|unsigned int\n4985|sizeof_leb128 (valueT value, int sign)\n4986|{\n4987|  if (sign)\n4988|    return sizeof_sleb128 ((offsetT) value);\n4989|  else\n4990|    return sizeof_uleb128 (value);\n4991|}\n4992|\n4993|/* Output a LEB128 value.
